# FLTMGR layout proof and live-register boundary

> **Evidence appendix.** I generated this formal report from the private evidence vault. Kernel virtual addresses are deterministic aliases, host paths are scrubbed, and private binaries, memory, peers, and secrets are not distributed. Module-relative RVAs, structure offsets, counts, hashes, and semantic boundaries are preserved.

<a id="private-evidence-reference"></a>

## Exact inputs

- Captured-build FLTMGR PDB: `<private-evidence-root>/<private-analysis>/memflow-process-dump/symbols/fltMgr-0D6729746A2247003272842381B0B618-1.pdb`
- PDB SHA-256: `b2258c4d743d5aff6007ebd659dbd919b1523d8af0bb34d3a4bd12ea749c1047`
- CodeView identity: `fltMgr.pdb`, GUID `0D6729746A2247003272842381B0B618`, age `1`
- Exact EAC raw PE SHA-256: `020d5da6b881408ced33be09b92dc45e12e641b8b3c91f3a327429e5027fef74`
- EAC PE binding: AMD64, timestamp `0x6a9054fa`, entry RVA `0x2594f8`, image size `0x2ae7000`, nine sections, security directory `0x2a93c00`/`0x5298`

## Captured-build type layouts

The following were read directly from the exact FLTMGR PDB with `llvm-pdbutil dump --types --type-index=<index> --dependents`.

| Type index | Type | Size | Fields used by the collector |
| --- | --- | ---: | --- |
| `0x17a2` | `_FLT_INSTANCE` | `0x3c0` | `Filter +0x48`, `FilterLink +0x78` |
| `0x18e0` | `_FLT_CALLBACK_DATA` | `0x58` | `Thread +0x08`, `Iopb +0x10`, `IoStatus +0x18`, `RequestorMode +0x50` |
| `0x1abe` | `_FLT_IO_PARAMETER_BLOCK` | `0x48` | `MajorFunction +0x04`, `TargetFileObject +0x08`, `TargetInstance +0x10`, `Parameters +0x18` |

This rules out an FLTMGR-layout mismatch as the reason the final six registered/body-compatible candidates failed EAC `TargetInstance` validation.

## Exact EAC register data flow at the recurring anchor

Disassembly of the exact PE at native body RVA `0x215cdd` establishes this sequence:

```text
0x215cdd  push r15
...
0x215ce6  push rdi
...
0x215d28  test r8,r8
0x215d31  mov  rdi,r8
0x215d34  mov  rax,[r8+0x10]
...
0x215d5b  cmp  byte ptr [rdi+0x50],0
0x215d65  mov  r15,[rdi+0x08]
...
0x215f00  call r8
0x215f03  test rax,rax
```

The three dereferences line up exactly with the captured-build `_FLT_CALLBACK_DATA` layout. Therefore the wrapper-transformed incoming `R8`, copied to nonvolatile `RDI`, is CallbackData in this native body. At RVA `0x215f03`, which dominated the final live anchors, CallbackData remains in `RDI`; the instruction is the return point after an indirect internal context-selection call, not a memory load of CallbackData.

The prologue saves the caller's incoming `RDI` before assigning CallbackData to `RDI`. It does not create a guaranteed outer-frame stack slot containing the new CallbackData value before `0x215f03`. A callee may temporarily preserve nonvolatile `RDI` in its own frame while the call is active, but a sampled return-address-bearing or saved KTHREAD stack is not sufficient on its own to prove that a nearby canonical qword is that live register value.

## Memflow-only register boundary

The collector is built against memflow `0.2.4`. Its `ConnectorCpuState` supplies `cpu_state`/`into_cpu_state`, but the `CpuState` trait implements only `pause` and `resume`. The source lists `read_register(s)` as TODO. Source file identity:

```text
<private-home>/.cargo/registry/src/index.crates.io-1949cf8c6b5b557f/memflow-0.2.4/src/connector/cpu_state.rs
SHA-256 0a5b2ceed76f506c30130020a12b26879f7ca74cda4cca3cee7d01da1b02633a
```

Consequently, the passive memflow-only collector cannot read live vCPU `RDI`. It instead observes paired-stable KPRCB `CurrentThread` values and bounded KTHREAD stack memory. For a currently executing thread, `KTHREAD.KernelStack` can describe scheduler-saved state rather than the instantaneous live vCPU stack/register state.

## Classification of the final near-hits

Observed exactly:

- Six paired-stable `_FLT_CALLBACK_DATA`/IOPB shapes were registered-major and body-compatible `IRP_MJ_CREATE` candidates one qword from the recurring `0x215f03` anchor.
- All six failed the bound exact EAC instance set.
- All six triggered an immediate exact EAC instance-list rewalk.
- All six were absent from that refreshed exact list.
- All six also failed the independent `_FLT_INSTANCE.Filter == exact EAC filter` relational check.

Therefore none is accepted as EAC CallbackData. Shared, nested, reused, or stale FLT state and an accidental structural match remain possible explanations; the capture does not distinguish among them. The exact conclusion is the failed EAC instance relationship, not a claim about which alternative produced the bytes.

## Explicit unobserved boundary

The following remain unobserved by this collector:

- Callback lifetimes entirely between samples.
- Instantaneous vCPU `RDI` and other live register values.
- Stack state outside the current-thread set, selected windows, anchor radius, or caps.
- Data that changed during any sequential read stage and was rejected by paired stability.
- A callback whose EAC execution existed but whose CallbackData pointer was never resident in a selected stack slot.

Zero validated rows is exact for the sampled, paired-stable, exact-instance-gated evidence. It is not evidence that EAC received zero minifilter callbacks during the run.

```

# Reachable RDMSR/WRMSR/RDPMC site closure

> **Evidence appendix.** I generated this formal report from the private evidence vault. Kernel virtual addresses are deterministic aliases, host paths are scrubbed, and private binaries, memory, peers, and secrets are not distributed. Module-relative RVAs, structure offsets, counts, hashes, and semantic boundaries are preserved.

<a id="private-evidence-reference"></a>

## Result

All 34 conservatively reachable MSR/counter-query sites in the exact captured image are now byte-validated and locally modeled: 15 RDMSR, 18 WRMSR, and one RDPMC. The raw image has 95 `0f 32`, 122 `0f 30`, and 62 `0f 33` byte pairs; raw-only pairs are retained separately and are not promoted to instructions.

## Exact protected-state handlers

- Nine RDMSR bodies take ECX from protected state dword `+0x190`, save their continuation from qword `+0x194`, return EAX/EDX in dwords `+0x190/+0x194`, set a success/fault qword to zero/one, and jump through the saved continuation. Seven additionally copy zero-extended EAX to qword `+0x198`; two use `+0x198` only as status.
- Fifteen WRMSR bodies load EAX/ECX/EDX from protected state dwords `+0x190/+0x194/+0x198`, load continuation qword `+0x19c`, write the MSR, preserve zero-extended EAX at qword `+0x190`, set status qword `+0x198` to zero or one, and jump through the continuation.
- The RDPMC body takes ECX from state dword `+0x190`, saves continuation qword `+0x194`, returns EAX/EDX into dwords `+0x190/+0x194`, sets status qword `+0x198`, and jumps through the saved continuation.

These are dynamic protected-machine micro-operations, not 25 fixed register probes. Their indices/selectors are supplied in the live state record.

## Exact interpreter and native paths

- RVAs `0x9040a/0x9069d` are the WRMSR/RDMSR operations inside the protected interpreter PDATA body `0x8c32e..0x92089`. Their MSR index is state dword `+0x10`; value halves use state `+0x8/+0x18`; both return to dispatcher `0x91d54`.
- `0xac537` is an entry-RCX-keyed RDMSR qword reader. `0x109a3c` is an entry-RCX-keyed low-byte predicate with an explicit exception fallback.
- Function `0x109a53..0x109b35` derives its key from `(RCX << 3) | RDX`, reads and saves one dynamic MSR, executes CLI, writes a masked value, reads it back for a transformed low-byte result, restores the exact saved EDX:EAX value, then executes STI. The four opcode sites are linked in the ledger.
- `0x21b560` is an R14-keyed fenced RDMSR reader with LFENCE immediately before and after, unresolved entry-key calls around the read, and a qword return assembled from EDX:EAX.

## Precision boundary

No conservatively reachable body embeds a closed fixed MSR index. Every index is protected-state, interpreter-state, or invocation-key derived. A same-epoch instruction/state observation is required to recover concrete runtime register numbers; none is invented here. Exact bytes and local dataflow do not establish live execution.

## Verification

- Unit tests: 7/7 pass.
- Statement coverage: 97.7%.
- Validation: `pass`.

```

```md
# Captured-build minifilter name/access policy at RVA 0x82dd2

> **Evidence appendix.** I generated this formal report from the private evidence vault. Kernel virtual addresses are deterministic aliases, host paths are scrubbed, and private binaries, memory, peers, and secrets are not distributed. Module-relative RVAs, structure offsets, counts, hashes, and semantic boundaries are preserved.

<a id="private-evidence-reference"></a>

## Closure statement

The 2,335-byte native policy body, all constant-key direct and split dispatches it reaches, its 41-record extension table, the native callback bodies, the Startup fallback, and the deny-snapshot helper are closed against the exact loaded image. Protected callback stubs are resolved to their exact delegate RVAs; their VM-mediated inner predicates remain explicitly unknown.

Evidence labels in this report follow `analysis.json`: exact resident bytes and arithmetic, derived-exact semantic reconstruction, inference, and unknown.

## Image identity

- Loaded image SHA-256: `cfc623de01a9c436c239c56a016d936a9070cdd2432596c06f75a3d88ae68081`
- Base: `KVA-0001`
- Size: `0x2ae7000`
- Entry: `0x82dd2..0x836f1`
- Entry seed / mix: `0x60460f80c282d33e` / `0xf1a6651f73d0aea8`
- Shared split key: `0xf409cdb9edccab83`
- Acquisition boundary: the already captured memflow loaded image only; this package performs no guest writes, hooks, injection, instrumentation, or request generation.

## Exact decision body

1. A valid caller supplies `(seed, desired_access, FLT_FILE_NAME_INFORMATION*, Thread*)`. The body resolves `IoGetFileObjectGenericMapping` and `RtlMapGenericMask`, then normalizes desired access in place.
2. It initializes the extension digest to resident SHA1(empty), `da39a3ee5e6b4b0d3255bfef95601890afd80709`. When `NamesParsed & 0x2`, `Extension.Buffer != NULL`, and `Extension.Length != 0`, it hashes the exact UTF-16LE extension bytes case-sensitively.
3. It linearly compares the 20-byte digest against 41 records at RVA `0x2fd460`, stride `0x90`.
4. On a match it enters a critical region, exclusively acquires the push lock at record `+0x20`, increments `+0x28` when mapped access intersects `0x89` (`FILE_READ_DATA/LIST_DIRECTORY | FILE_READ_EA | FILE_READ_ATTRIBUTES`), increments `+0x2c` when access intersects `0x10116` (`FILE_WRITE_DATA/ADD_FILE | FILE_APPEND_DATA/ADD_SUBDIRECTORY | FILE_WRITE_EA | FILE_WRITE_ATTRIBUTES | DELETE`), invokes the callback at `+0x18`, releases the lock, and leaves the critical region.
5. Callback result `1` invokes RVA `0x836f1` for the deny snapshot and returns `1`; every other callback result returns `2`.
6. If no record matches and mutation access is present, it applies the decoded Startup parent-component predicate described below; a match returns `1`, otherwise `2`.

The valid-input return contract is exact in the outer consumer: `1` is the deny decision and `2` is the no-deny decision. The `NameInfo == NULL` epilogue path restores an uninitialized `r13d`, so no stable value is assigned to that invalid-caller path.

## Main dispatch closure

| Call RVA | Role | Mechanism | Resolved target | Export alias |
|---:|---|---|---|---|
| `0x82eb8` | resolve_generic_mapping | direct_keyed_resolver | `0x16a9a0` | internal EAC RVA |
| `0x82ef7` | generic_mapping | split_pointer_dispatch | `KVA-0081` | IoGetFileObjectGenericMapping |
| `0x82f4b` | resolve_map_generic_access | direct_keyed_resolver | `0x16a9a0` | internal EAC RVA |
| `0x82f90` | map_generic_access | split_pointer_dispatch | `KVA-0082` | RtlMapGenericMask |
| `0x830c4` | sha1_extension_update | direct_keyed_dispatch | `0xaa94` | internal EAC RVA |
| `0x83108` | sha1_extension_final | direct_keyed_dispatch | `0xacb8` | internal EAC RVA |
| `0x83217` | resolve_critical_region_enter | direct_keyed_resolver | `0x16a9a0` | internal EAC RVA |
| `0x83256` | critical_region_enter | split_pointer_dispatch | `KVA-0083` | KeEnterCriticalRegion |
| `0x832a1` | resolve_push_lock_acquire | direct_keyed_resolver | `0x16a9a0` | internal EAC RVA |
| `0x832e5` | push_lock_acquire | split_pointer_dispatch | `KVA-0084` | ExAcquirePushLockExclusiveEx |
| `0x8337b` | resolve_push_lock_release | direct_keyed_resolver | `0x16a9a0` | internal EAC RVA |
| `0x833bf` | push_lock_release | split_pointer_dispatch | `KVA-0085` | ExReleasePushLockExclusiveEx |
| `0x83407` | resolve_critical_region_leave | direct_keyed_resolver | `0x16a9a0` | internal EAC RVA |
| `0x83446` | critical_region_leave | split_pointer_dispatch | `KVA-0086` | KeLeaveCriticalRegion |
| `0x834a6` | deny_snapshot | direct_keyed_dispatch | `0x836f1` | internal EAC RVA |
| `0x836ac` | parent_component_match | direct_keyed_dispatch | `0x81fd8` | internal EAC RVA |

Every external target above maps by exact VA equality to exports in captured `ntoskrnl.exe` (SHA-256 `a0c6136387783d0ee217dc69df4301c7a45e87efcbda2fa7937224fc1df61c27`). Each split row in `analysis.json` also preserves both pointer-table RVAs, both resolved source RVAs and qwords, the intermediate split pointer, low component, high component, shift, and modulus.

The fixed relative calls are also closed: main-body `0x82fe6/0x8304b → memmove`, `0x83075/0x83547/0x836b9 → memset`, and `0x83197 → memcmp`; helper `0x83919/0x83c10 → memset` and `0x8395f/0x83cba → memmove`. Their resident targets are exactly `0x259000`, `0x2592c0`, and `0x258f00` respectively.

The exact `FLT_FILE_NAME_INFORMATION` offsets consumed are `NamesParsed +0x02`; `Name.Length/+0x08`, `Name.MaximumLength/+0x0a`, `Name.Buffer/+0x10`; `Extension.Length/+0x38`, `Extension.Buffer/+0x40`; and `ParentDir.Length/+0x68`, `ParentDir.Buffer/+0x70`.

## Extension transformation and table

The state source at RVA `0x2eac2c` is `0123456789abcdeffedcba9876543210f0e1d2c3` and the default digest source at `0x2bcaa8` is SHA1(empty). RVA `0xaa94` performs extension streaming, RVA `0xacb8` finalizes, RVA `0xb068` is the stream-update implementation, and RVA `0xb276` is the compression implementation. Every listed preimage below was independently re-hashed with SHA1 over UTF-16LE and compared to the exact resident record bytes.

The exact callback partition is:

- Native access-mask callback `0x808ff`: `bmp`, `dll`, `exe`, `lnk`. It returns `1` iff mapped access intersects `0x10116`; otherwise `2`.
- Native unconditional callback `0x809b2`: the 27 rows shown with `always_return_1`.
- Protected common stub `0x224c7e → 0x3b854b`: `png`, `zip`, `jpg`, `jpeg`, `mp4`, `avi`, `gz`.
- Protected dedicated stubs: `db: 0x224cb5 → 0x3da1d9`, `txt: 0x224ced → 0x38d0f0`, and empty/no extension: `0x224d20 → 0x4034d8`.

The `avi` membership is taken directly from record 14's callback pointer and is covered by a regression test.

## Record layout and mutations

| Offset | Size | Type | Meaning |
|---:|---:|---|---|
| `0x0` | 20 | bytes | SHA1(extension UTF-16LE) |
| `0x14` | 4 | padding | zero in capture |
| `0x18` | 8 | function pointer | policy callback |
| `0x20` | 8 | EX_PUSH_LOCK | per-record lock |
| `0x28` | 4 | u32 | mapped_access & 0x89 count |
| `0x2c` | 4 | u32 | mapped_access & 0x10116 count |
| `0x30` | 4 | u32 | callback-private mutable field |
| `0x34` | 4 | u32 | deny snapshot call count |
| `0x38` | 2 | u16 | retained Name.Length |
| `0x3a` | 2 | u16 | retained buffer capacity |
| `0x40` | 8 | wchar pointer | retained name buffer |
| `0x48` | 64 | qword[8] | RtlWalkFrameChain snapshot |
| `0x88` | 4 | u32 | derived process-context age in ms |
| `0x8c` | 4 | u32 | original desired access snapshot |

All 41 captured mutable record states are preserved per row in `analysis.json`. These are captured live values, not claimed initialization values. In particular, the protected common callback rows contain nonzero runtime counters; field `+0x30` is proven mutable but its private meaning is not recovered.

## Startup fallback

The body decodes the exact 94-byte source at `0x2bcabc` to `\Microsoft\Windows\Start Menu\Programs\Startup` plus a UTF-16 NUL. The transformation is 23 little-endian dwords XORed with a rolling state starting at `0x1257ed81`; each step applies `state = ROL32(state*0x41c64e6d + 0x3039, 28)`. The final two bytes are XORed with successive bytes of `0xa253639f`.

The fallback requires mapped access `& 0x10116`, `NamesParsed & 0x8`, a non-null ParentDir buffer, and nonzero ParentDir length. RVA `0xd1839` performs a bounded UTF-16 substring scan with `(candidate ^ needle) & 0xffdf == 0`, giving ASCII-letter case insensitivity for this literal. RVA `0x81fd8` additionally requires the following code unit to be NUL or backslash.

## Deny snapshot helper

RVA `0x836f1` increments record `+0x34`, acquires the same per-record push lock, and refreshes immediately when retained-path metadata is incomplete. Once a retained path exists, refresh is sampled only when PRNG result modulo 100 is zero.

A refresh clears 72 bytes at record `+0x48`, conditionally copies `Name.MaximumLength` bytes to the retained path buffer while storing `Name.Length`, resolves Thread→Process→PID, reads process-context `+0x218`, computes `(KeQueryTimeIncrement()*qword[KVA-0087]-timestamp)/10000`, records 8 frames with flags 1, copies 64 frame bytes to record `+0x48`, and stores original desired access at `+0x8c`. Lock release and critical-region exit occur on all local paths. The caller ignores the helper's return.

### Helper dispatch closure

| Call RVA | Role | Mechanism | Resolved target | Export alias |
|---:|---|---|---|---|
| `0x837aa` | resolve_critical_region_enter | direct_keyed_resolver | `0x16a9a0` | internal EAC RVA |
| `0x837e9` | critical_region_enter | split_pointer_dispatch | `KVA-0083` | KeEnterCriticalRegion |
| `0x83830` | resolve_push_lock_acquire | direct_keyed_resolver | `0x16a9a0` | internal EAC RVA |
| `0x83874` | push_lock_acquire | split_pointer_dispatch | `KVA-0084` | ExAcquirePushLockExclusiveEx |

| `0x839b1` | resolve_thread_to_process | direct_keyed_resolver | `0x16a9a0` | internal EAC RVA |

| `0x83a47` | resolve_process_id | direct_keyed_resolver | `0x16a9a0` | internal EAC RVA |

| `0x83ac5` | process_context_lookup | direct_keyed_dispatch | `0xdfce7` | internal EAC RVA |

The nested PRNG at `0x12efd2` uses state RVA `0x305160`, seeds a zero state from RDTSC low32, reuses split key `0xf409cdb9edccab83`, and resolves `RtlRandomEx` exactly.

## Explicit recovery boundaries

- **protected delegate bodies at RVAs 0x3b854b, 0x3da1d9, 0x38d0f0, 0x4034d8** — known: exact table membership and direct stub targets; unknown: inner return predicates and mutations after the VM/context-save bridge.
- **NameInfo == NULL branch at RVA 0x82e24** — known: branches to epilogue; unknown: stable return value because restored r13d is not initialized on that invalid-caller path.
- **record+0x30 callback-private u32** — known: exact offset and captured values; unknown: semantic purpose and protected-callback update rule.
- **process context lookup/release helpers** — known: exact targets, keys, PID input, context+0x218 read, and release call; unknown: full context object schema and lookup ownership internals.

These boundaries are not filled with behavioral guesses. Everything outside them in this report is backed by exact bytes, resolver arithmetic, export equality, or a deterministic derived-exact model checked against those bytes.

`appendices/native-resolver-census.md`:

```md
# EAC native resolver census

> **Evidence appendix.** I generated this formal report from the private evidence vault. Kernel virtual addresses are deterministic aliases, host paths are scrubbed, and private binaries, memory, peers, and secrets are not distributed. Module-relative RVAs, structure offsets, counts, hashes, and semantic boundaries are preserved.

<a id="private-evidence-reference"></a>

## Result

The native `.text` census contains exactly **10** direct calls to the encoded-pointer primitive at RVA `0x16a9a0`. Eight have invariant RCX/RDX/R8 inputs and two load RCX from caller-frame state. In the primary captured resolver state, the eight invariant sites collapse to **5** exact NT export addresses.

This extends the earlier four protected-prefix mappings; it does not replace them. No sample instruction was executed and no guest instrumentation was used.

## Exact primary-state native mappings

| Target | Native resolver call RVAs | Runtime execution seen? |
|---|---:|---|
| `ntoskrnl.exe!IoGetCurrentProcess`, `ntoskrnl.exe!PsGetCurrentProcess` | `0x1e9ddb` | no; exact loaded-state arithmetic only |
| `ntoskrnl.exe!ExGetPreviousMode`, `ntoskrnl.exe!PsGetCurrentThreadPreviousMode` | `0x1e9da4` | no; exact loaded-state arithmetic only |
| `ntoskrnl.exe!__C_specific_handler` | `0xb8c71` | no; exact loaded-state arithmetic only |
| `ntoskrnl.exe!_vsnwprintf` | `0xa5fd1`, `0xa9294`, `0xfa27e`, `0x15b96e` | no; exact loaded-state arithmetic only |
| `ntoskrnl.exe!_vsnprintf` | `0xe9d9d` | no; exact loaded-state arithmetic only |

The four repeated wide-format sites resolve to `_vsnwprintf`; the other invariant sites resolve to `__C_specific_handler`, `_vsnprintf`, the `ExGetPreviousMode`/`PsGetCurrentThreadPreviousMode` alias address, and the `IoGetCurrentProcess`/`PsGetCurrentProcess` alias address.

## Context-keyed calls

- `0xd4728` in PDATA function `0xd46f6..0xd475f` loads the resolver key from `qword ptr [rbp + 0x60]` and invokes the decoded target with `call rax` at `0xd474b`. The key value and callee identity remain exact capture blockers.
- `0x11f971` in PDATA function `0x11f93b..0x11f9b1` loads the resolver key from `qword ptr [rbp + 0x108]` and invokes the decoded target with `call rax` at `0x11f998`. The key value and callee identity remain exact capture blockers.

## Cross-load result

The two loaded images have the same exact EAC PE tuple, and the two NT images have the same PE and CodeView identity. All ten call instructions, their argument-source RVAs, and their multiply/XOR transforms are fixed. All 20 logical resident RDX/R8 operand values rotate across the loads (14 unique source RVAs), as does the modulus state. Recomputing each pipeline with the state captured from its own load produces the same normalized NT module RVA and exact export identity for all eight invariant sites. This establishes exact two-load decoded equality, but it remains loaded-state arithmetic rather than call-execution evidence; a lifecycle sampler is still needed to bind state epochs to actual use.
The fixed-code claim is byte-backed: the complete PDATA ranges for RVA `0x16a9a0` and both modular-arithmetic helpers, plus every recovered RCX/RDX/R8 setup and post-transform instruction through the indirect dispatch, are equal across both loaded images. Their range hashes and instruction bytes are retained in `native-resolver-ledger.json`.

## Resident pointer census

There are **1627** aligned pointers in non-executable EAC sections whose `(module, module RVA)` projection is exactly equal across both loads. Their module counts are:

- `EasyAntiCheat_EOS.sys`: 1541
- `FLTMGR.SYS`: 1
- `mouclass.sys`: 1
- `ntoskrnl.exe`: 84

These rows are stored in `cross-load-resident-pointers.jsonl`. Presence is not treated as a call, dereference, or behavior claim.

## Evidence boundaries

- A primary loaded-state target that equals an export is an exact arithmetic mapping for that captured state, not proof that the call site executed during capture.
- Across two independent loads, all 20 logical resident RDX/R8 operands rotate while their 14 source RVAs remain fixed; using each load's own captured state reproduces the same normalized NT export identity for all eight invariant sites.
- Cross-load decoded equality establishes deterministic loaded-state arithmetic for two observed boots, not proof that any native call site executed during either capture.
- The two frame-keyed sites cannot be named until their runtime RCX keys are captured or derived from all callers.
- Aligned resident pointers establish exact stored values only; they do not establish dereference, execution, or purpose.
- The protected ledger covers recovered entry, dispatch, and notify prefixes only; it is not every protected resolver invocation in the 42 MiB virtualized section.

## Reproduce

Run `python3 build_native_resolver_census.py` with the two captured EAC images, their module ledgers, both NT images, and the phase-1/phase-2 evidence paths shown by `--help`. Then run `python3 -m unittest -v test_build_native_resolver_census.py`.

```

`appendices/native-resolver-lifecycle.md`:

```md
# EAC live native-resolver validation

> **Evidence appendix.** I generated this formal report from the private evidence vault. Kernel virtual addresses are deterministic aliases, host paths are scrubbed, and private binaries, memory, peers, and secrets are not distributed. Module-relative RVAs, structure offsets, counts, hashes, and semantic boundaries are preserved.

<a id="private-evidence-reference"></a>

## Result

The completed memflow capture contains **204650** contiguous samples over **420.000630 seconds**. All **165890** stable loaded-state samples resolved all eight invariant pipelines to their exact build-matched NT export RVAs. There were **815** recorded errors and **3** resolver-state epochs.

The arithmetic was recomputed independently from the raw epoch operands and modulus. All eight results imply one common, non-persisted live NT base, and every normalized RVA and alias agrees with the current canonical census.
Across every resolver-state epoch and binding generation with a stable state, the eight normalized NT target RVAs, exact export aliases, and indirect dispatch forms are byte-for-byte equal.

## Integrity closure

Every payload file is covered by the self-excluding manifest; `SHA256SUMS` is the exact ordered projection of those entries plus the manifest digest; `run.complete.json` commits the manifest and summary digests; `run.incomplete.json` is absent; no extra payload files, symlinks, or group/world permissions exist.

This is mutation-detection closure, not an external signature or trusted timestamp.

## Binding lifecycle

The event/sample state machine closed exactly across **6** binding transitions, **1** fail-closed invalidations, **4** binding generations, and **3** resolver-state epochs.

Driver absence observed: **YES**. Unload/reload observed: **YES**. Refresh failure observed: **YES**.

The complete sealed package also contains no textual canonical x64 guest-VA literals, so its package-level no-VA claim passes.

`appendices/object-access-policy.md`:

```md
# Captured-build EAC OB process/thread handle-policy semantics

> **Evidence appendix.** I generated this formal report from the private evidence vault. Kernel virtual addresses are deterministic aliases, host paths are scrubbed, and private binaries, memory, peers, and secrets are not distributed. Module-relative RVAs, structure offsets, counts, hashes, and semantic boundaries are preserved.

<a id="private-evidence-reference"></a>

## Closure statement

I closed the process and thread pre/post handle-policy paths for the exact captured loaded image, including the protected veneers at RVAs `0xeab72`, `0x7eb1a`, and `0xeb13b`, their exact caller ABIs, DesiredAccess flow, reachable native/internal calls, post gates, state layouts, and bounded cleanup paths. The recovered access transforms are both identity: this captured build does not remove any access bit in these helpers. The process mask `0x000e0eba` selects extra bookkeeping only.

I also corrected a prior attribution: normal `0xeb13b` does not call `0x1cd5a8`, `0xeb35f`, or `0xe0314`. Its protected root writes zero to a local, later reads the same address through a relocated-frame alias, and takes the short return after the single `0x1b092b` lookup. The exact differential witness and both byte regions are frozen in this package.

Evidence labels are strict: `observed_exact` is resident bytes/PE metadata/export equality; `derived_exact` is deterministic control/data flow over those bytes; `synthetic_witness` is a controlled decoded-instruction path, not live state; `unknown` is an explicit capture boundary.

## Phase-3/Phase-4 claim reconciliation

I hash- and schema-bound the earlier Phase-3 OB ledger and both Phase-4 closure ledgers before comparing their claims with this byte-closed lift. The following prior claims remain confirmed:

- Phase 3 ABI offsets, exact PsProcessType/PsThreadType discrimination, current-vs-duplicate process selection, OriginalDesiredAccess non-read, CallContext non-write, and zero pre-callback result are confirmed.
- Phase 3 exact veneers 0xea9b9->0x43fd46 and 0xebd81->0x41c5e7 are confirmed.
- Phase 4 correctly retained U4-OB-HELPERS as unresolved rather than assigning access-bit semantics.

The following claims are explicitly superseded rather than silently inherited:

- Phase 3 attributed the process DesiredAccess write to 0x7eb1a EAX; exact distinct-marker interpretation proves 0xeab72 EAX is written and the 0x7eb1a return is ignored.
- Phase 3 described the post status gate as nonzero failure; exact branch recovery proves NT_SUCCESS(ReturnStatus), so positive nonzero statuses continue.
- Phase 3 placed PID/context work on the ordinary post success path; exact equality recovery shows it is behind helper_eax != GrantedAccess and is therefore infeasible with the captured identity helpers.
- The earlier working model attributed 0x1cd5a8 and 0xeb35f to normal 0xeb13b; exact self-zero alias recovery disproves that attribution.

The Phase-3 source is frozen as `f52661cdad041dd5cc51e49d08c968dc02c8a9e368e05009adb340a365e88122`; Phase-4 unresolved and coverage sources are `12311aa204990a402ca1abeb057fe3eaba20534926c24f16f2fc2870d6de75b3` and `aea512f7b985837aeed27b5a1a74b43dab794a57a536238bab7eb995ee538070`. Phase-4 item `U4-OB-HELPERS` is closed for this captured image. Runtime-populated out-of-image vectors and lists remain bounded unknowns, not helper-semantic gaps.

## Image and acquisition boundary

- Loaded image: `<private-capture-01>/kernel-survey-live/kernel-images/KVA-0003-EasyAntiCheat_EOS.sys-2ae7000.loaded.bin`
- Base / Size: `KVA-0001` / `0x2ae7000`
- SHA-256: `cfc623de01a9c436c239c56a016d936a9070cdd2432596c06f75a3d88ae68081`
- All work is host-side over the already captured memflow image. No captured driver instruction is executed. No guest write, hook, injection, request generation, or instrumentation occurs.

## Callback ABI and exact outer behavior

The pre callback consumes `POB_PRE_OPERATION_INFORMATION*` in RDX. The recovered offsets are Operation `+0x00`, KernelHandle flag `+0x04 bit 0`, Object `+0x08`, ObjectType `+0x10`, CallContext `+0x18`, Parameters `+0x20`, DesiredAccess `Parameters+0x00`, OriginalDesiredAccess `Parameters+0x04`, and duplicate TargetProcess `Parameters+0x10`.

For handle create (`Operation == 1`) it selects `IoGetCurrentProcess/PsGetCurrentProcess`; for duplicate it selects `DuplicateHandleInformation.TargetProcess`. It compares ObjectType by exact equality with captured `*PsProcessType` and `*PsThreadType`. Other types are untouched. OriginalDesiredAccess is not read, CallContext is not written, and the callback returns `OB_PREOP_SUCCESS (0)`.

For a process object, the pre callback calls `0xeab72`, writes EAX to DesiredAccess, then calls `0x7eb1a`; the follow-up return is ignored. For a thread object it writes the EAX result of `0xeb13b`. The differential markers prove the process write comes from the first helper rather than the follow-up.

## Process primary helper: 0xeab72 -> 0x370ff0

Exact pre ABI: RCX=`0x62266c40473f9301` (post uses `0x09b4e5d2d9bc1d01`); only DL of RDX is semantically initialized to KernelHandle; R8=`0xa204`; R9=Object; caller stack `+0x20` supplies the access DWORD; `+0x28` is `0x680fa1b8f7ac`; `+0x30` is the selected/current process. The return EAX is always the original access DWORD.

The helper always calls `0xeb35f(key=0xbff887bea9c789e6, &local_context, Object)` and releases a non-null local context through `0xe0314`. When `(access & 0x000e0eba) != 0`, it additionally obtains `PsGetProcessId(Object)`, acquires the fast/guarded mutex at EAC VA `KVA-0071`, performs association payload lookup `0x1b18bf`, object query `0x86953`, association insert/update `0x1b11e6`, and releases the mutex. None of those outcomes is used as the access result.

The exact side-effect mask contains these bits:

- `0x00000002 PROCESS_CREATE_THREAD`
- `0x00000008 PROCESS_VM_OPERATION`
- `0x00000010 PROCESS_VM_READ`
- `0x00000020 PROCESS_VM_WRITE`
- `0x00000080 PROCESS_CREATE_PROCESS`
- `0x00000200 PROCESS_SET_INFORMATION`
- `0x00000400 PROCESS_QUERY_INFORMATION`
- `0x00000800 PROCESS_SUSPEND_RESUME`
- `0x00020000 READ_CONTROL`
- `0x00040000 WRITE_DAC`
- `0x00080000 WRITE_OWNER`

The 32-entry single-bit matrix in `vm-evidence.json` confirms the same mask, while both interpreted helper witnesses (`0x1000` short and `0x2` extended) return their inputs unchanged.

## Process association follow-up: 0x7eb1a -> 0x3d4178

Exact ABI: RCX is opaque caller state, RDX is selected/current process, R8D is Original DesiredAccess, R9D is effective access returned by `0xeab72`, and caller stack `+0x28` supplies Object. The helper first calls `PsGetProcessId(Object)` and `0xdfce7(key=0xa104faae6e064963, PID)`. A NULL context returns immediately.

For a non-null context it reads the byte at `context+0x4b4` and association-state pointer at `context+0xd8`. When `+0x4b4` is zero it also queries `PsGetProcessId(selected process)` before forming the runtime key. It calls `0x1b1cb0(key_high, key_low, selected process, state)`. A membership hit skips audit/insert. A miss calls `0x7d871(effective, selected process, opaque key, original, context)` and then `0x1b11e6(0, state, selected process, 0, opaque seed)`. Because that insertion has zero payload, native `0x1b11e6` accepts it only when `state+0x39 != 0`. Every non-null context is released with `0xe0314`. The pre callback ignores this helper's scalar return.

The key combine in native `0x1b1cb0` is exactly `((RCX << 42) | RDX) mod 2^64`, but the two components depend on runtime context/vector state. No fixed production key is claimed; concrete values in `vm-evidence.json` are labeled synthetic.

## Thread primary helper: 0xeb13b -> 0x42cf3b

Exact ABI: RCX=Object, RDX=selected/current process, R8=`0x73e4da5e6bf6ad6f`, only R9B is semantically initialized to KernelHandle, and caller stack `+0x20` supplies the access DWORD. It calls `0x1b092b(key=0x617b3c3eb515eb29, selected/current process)`, whose captured initialized cache resolves to resident DWORD `0x220`; it reads `*(Object+0x220)` as owner process and compares that owner with the selected process.

The protected root had previously been over-attributed. At RVA `0x2083b67` it writes zero to `[saved_original_rsp-0x68]`; after the call/context relocation, RVA `0x1960997` reads the same address as `[relocated_saved_rsp+0x30]`. The zero test forces the short epilogue. Across exact caller-state seeds, the complete normal call sequence is save-context, `0x1b092b`, restore-context. EAX is the original access DWORD.

## Post callback and unreachable normal event tail

The post callback consumes Operation `+0x00`, KernelHandle `+0x04`, Object `+0x08`, ObjectType `+0x10`, CallContext `+0x18`, ReturnStatus `+0x20`, Parameters `+0x28`, and GrantedAccess at `Parameters+0x00`. It returns VOID and never writes the OB information structure.

It calls current-process lookup before its gates. Only handle create (`Operation == 1`) proceeds; duplicate exits. Its status condition is `NT_SUCCESS(ReturnStatus)`—the sign bit must be clear—not equality with zero. Therefore positive informational/success statuses proceed and `0x80000000`/`0xc0000001` exit.

On process/thread create success it invokes the corresponding identity helper and compares helper EAX with original GrantedAccess. The later event tail is selected only on inequality. Because both recovered helpers return GrantedAccess unchanged, that tail is infeasible on this build's normal path.

The byte-present counterfactual delta tail is nevertheless closed: process obtains Object PID, calls `0xdfce7`, queries current thread ID when a context exists, invokes `0x1da3b4(0, context, 0x10, opaque)`, and releases through `0xe0314`; thread obtains owner via `Object+0x220`, obtains owner PID, then performs the same lookup/emit/release without querying current thread ID. `0x1da3b4 -> 0x402ef0` calls `0x1db657 -> 0x3c3436`; the inner path zeros a 0x100-byte record with `memset`, reads `context+0x324`, calls native emitter `0x1ef982`, and releases an additional context reference.

## Nested association/audit tail

The membership-miss tail `0x7d871 -> 0x3db76e` calls native `0x1f9c9d`, protected `0x130ed5 -> 0x3a6f7d`, native `0x130bde`, four generic resolver operations, protected `0x8a738 -> 0x44163c`, and acquire/release of the mutex at `context+0x418`. The process-facts helper calls captured exports `PsGetProcessWow64Process` and `PsGetProcessId`; the token helper calls `PsReferencePrimaryToken`. All three protected wrappers return zero on the controlled exact call-state witness, and their scalar returns are not access decisions.

The token acquisition is exact. Its matching dereference/terminal owner crosses resolver-dependent native record handling at `0x130bde`; the unavailable target vectors prevent naming that release without inventing evidence.

## Native structure layouts and lifetimes

- Context lookup `0xdfce7`: object/PID key `+0x3a8`, encoded reference `+0x668`, retired flag `+0x66c`, LIST_ENTRY `+0x670/+0x678`; success requires matching identity and `+0x66c == 0`, then takes an encoded reference.
- Context release `0xe0314`: live path performs encoded subtract at `+0x668`; retired final-reference path validates/unlinks `+0x670/+0x678`, releases nested `+0x58/+0x370/+0x4d0` state, clears with `memset`, and reaches tail cleanup.
- Association table `0x1b1cb0/0x1b11e6/0x1b18bf`: mode `+0x38`, insertion policy `+0x39`, mask `+0x3c`, buckets `+0x40`, count `+0x48`; node next `+0x00`, key `+0x08`.
- `0x1b11e6` optionally copies payload with captured resident `memmove` at `0x259000`; clear paths use `memset` at `0x2592c0`. The task-supplied resident primitive identity `0x258f00=memcmp` is retained as authority, though no recovered normal target path consumes it.
- Every non-null follow-up context reaches `0xe0314`. Post delta-tail context references are absent on the exact identity-result path; the counterfactual nested emitter takes and releases its extra reference.

## Explicit remaining-work ledger

| ID | Exact known boundary | Still unknown | Evidence needed to close |
|---|---|---|---|
| `protected_resolver_vectors_outside_image` | VM anchor is image_base-0x7610; referenced slots anchor+0x314ad0/+0x314ad8 and +0x314d90/+0x314d98 resolve to RVAs 0x30d4c0/0x30d4c8 and 0x30d780/0x30d788, all beyond SizeOfImage 0x2ae7000 | the native identities behind several eab72, 7eb1a, 7d871, and post-emitter calls cannot be mapped from this exact image | Passive memflow capture of the named runtime page/object while preserving this exact module identity |

| `no_live_branch_observation` | all semantics are offline lifts and differential concrete interpretation of captured bytes | branch frequency and live EAC state values are not claimed | Passive memflow capture of the named runtime page/object while preserving this exact module identity |

Those are capture boundaries, not behavioral gaps hidden behind a probable label. The resolver slots at effective RVAs `0x30d4c0/0x30d4c8/0x30d780/0x30d788`, list heads/nodes outside SizeOfImage, live association contents, final emitter sink, and primary-token terminal owner cannot be recovered from bytes that are absent from this image. Branch frequency is likewise not inferred from offline witnesses.

## Reproduction and verification

The frozen VM evidence schema is `eac-ob-vm-differential-evidence-v1`. Its pre attribution, short/extended process paths, corrected thread short path, context-null/hit/miss process follow-up paths, nested audit children, all 32 mask bits, NTSTATUS boundary, duplicate exit, exact identity exits, and forced delta-only tails are regression checked.

The 14-test suite passes, and `COVERAGE.json` records 89.8% weighted line coverage across the three package core modules, above the 80.0% threshold. The measurement command is frozen in that file and uses only Python's standard-library tracer.

Run `python analysis/ob-access-policy-semantics/validate.py` to recompute the semantic analysis, VM evidence, exact byte slices, export mappings, and package manifest. Run `python analysis/ob-access-policy-semantics/verify_manifest.py` for the immutable inventory/digest check only.

`appendices/privileged-sites.md`:

```md
# Remaining reachable privileged/transition site closure

> **Evidence appendix.** I generated this formal report from the private evidence vault. Kernel virtual addresses are deterministic aliases, host paths are scrubbed, and private binaries, memory, peers, and secrets are not distributed. Module-relative RVAs, structure offsets, counts, hashes, and semantic boundaries are preserved.

<a id="private-evidence-reference"></a>

## Result

All 15 conservatively reachable sites outside the separately closed CPUID/XGETBV, RDMSR/WRMSR/RDPMC, and RDTSC families are byte-validated and locally classified in the exact captured image. The closed set is four OUTs, SIDT, SGDT, two WBINVDs, INVD, XBEGIN, XEND, VMREAD, SLDT, SMSW, and STR.

## Protected interpreter operations

- `0x8e8a8` is SIDT inside PDATA `0x8c32e..0x92089`. It calls the interpreter address resolver with RCX=`[local+0x7e8]`, RDX=`[local+0x678] XOR [local+0x7d8]`, R8=RDI, then writes the 10-byte IDTR pseudo-descriptor to the resolver-returned RAX address and jumps to dispatcher `0x91d54`.
- `0x8eebe` is the identical address-resolution path followed by SGDT, writing the 10-byte GDTR pseudo-descriptor, then jumping to the same dispatcher.
- `0x8eae9` is an operand-free WBINVD interpreter operation followed directly by the shared-dispatcher jump.

The exact destination addresses and descriptor bytes remain live-interpreter-state values; the image alone does not supply them.

## Native keyed OUT bodies

PDATA `0x158a20..0x158c69` takes RCX32 as an input mask/value, DL as a mode flag, R8 as a descriptor pointer, and R9 as a key. It reads the descriptor tag at `+0x1004` and bytes `+0..2`, invokes two entry-key-decoded helpers, and conditionally executes:

- `0x158c45`: `OUT DX,EAX`, with DX=`low16((K32*-714) XOR 0x23a4)` and EAX the exact keyed OR-composition recorded in the ledger.
- `0x158c54`: `OUT DX,AX`, with DX=`low16((K32*531) XOR 0x7fa2)` and AX selected from the low 16 bits of the helper/default value, RCX32 mask, and DL mode. The function returns the full 32-bit selected value: a zero-extended helper word on helper success, otherwise the full keyed default expression.

PDATA `0x158c6a..0x158f07` takes descriptor address `RCX OR (R9<<39)`, RDX as key, R8B as enable/data, and its fifth stack argument as mode. R8B zero exits before either OUT. Its gated fallback executes:

- `0x158eda`: `OUT DX,EAX`, with DX=`low16((K32*-4448) XOR 0xfcf8)` and EAX the exact descriptor-byte/key composition in the ledger.
- `0x158ef0`: `OUT DX,AX`, with DX=`low16(R8B OR ((K32*-22078) XOR 0xffff9ffc))` and AX the exact selected-value/mask/mode composition. The function's normal return is the separately saved zero-extended word from the first decoded helper's output buffer; R8B zero returns zero before helper or port activity.

Both bodies use the same closed 64-bit key mixer. No fixed port is embedded: the formulas are exact and concrete DX/EAX/AX values require the invocation key, descriptor bytes, and decoded-helper results. No local OUT-fault recovery is visible.

## Native transition and cache helpers

- `0x237b90..0x237b99` is an empty RTM transaction probe: XBEGIN aborts to the RET at `0x237b99` with status in EAX; success reaches XEND at `0x237b96`, commits, and returns with entry RAX unchanged. Unsupported-instruction faults have no local handler or PDATA coverage.
- `0x237c20..0x237c31` is a VMREAD status helper. Its paired JZ/JNZ always converge on `VMREAD [RDX],RCX`; the skipped `48 35` bytes are dead. Success writes the selected VMCS field to qword `[RDX]`. `SETE AL; SETB CL; ADC AL,CL` returns 0 for success, 1 for VMfailValid, and 2 for VMfailInvalid.
- PDATA `0x237c83..0x237c9d` always executes WBINVD, writes RDX verbatim to qword `[RCX]`, executes INVD, and returns. Its paired JZ/JNZ likewise skips a two-byte opaque prefix.

## Descriptor-register leaf queries

- `0x238050`: SLDT AX, then RET.
- `0x238060`: SMSW AX, then RET.
- `0x238070`: STR AX, then RET.

Each changes only AX; the upper 48 bits of RAX remain the entry value. None has PDATA or a local exception path. The raw image contains a second exact `STR AX` pattern, but only `0x238070` is in the conservative reachable inventory, so the other pattern is not promoted.

## Evidence boundary

This package establishes exact image identity, opcode bytes, local-window hashes, PDATA containment, register/state formulas, continuation behavior, and visible local exception boundaries. It does not claim that any site executed during the capture epoch. Runtime keys, interpreter destinations, branch/helper outcomes, VMCS fields, selector values, and actual descriptor bytes require same-epoch state evidence.

## Verification

- Unit tests: 7/7 pass.
- Statement coverage: 95.4%.
- Validation: `pass`.

```

`appendices/process-timeline.md`:

```md
# EAC/Fortnite focused-process timeline and byte-recovery report

> **Evidence appendix.** I generated this formal report from the private evidence vault. Kernel virtual addresses are deterministic aliases, host paths are scrubbed, and private binaries, memory, peers, and secrets are not distributed. Module-relative RVAs, structure offsets, counts, hashes, and semantic boundaries are preserved.

<a id="private-evidence-reference"></a>

## Scope and evidence vocabulary

This report covers the sealed, host-only memflow capture at `<private-capture-01>/focused-process-watch/`, with the independent second memflow session at `<private-capture-02>/` used only where explicitly labelled as later confirmation. No guest command, API hook, packet trace, syscall trace, call stack, debugger, or atomic guest snapshot contributed to the focused-process evidence.

The terms in this report are deliberately strict:

- **Exact bytes** are bytes read from a sealed content-addressed memflow blob or a recovered fragment with recorded provenance. Released non-page-identical fragments carry public SHA-256 values; page-identical fragments are withheld and referenced only by canonical source-set ordinal. Those ordinals are assigned from the two explicit `--source-capture` authorities sorted by basename and resolved path, then exact ledger order; exact page paths, digests, and authority-ledger hashes remain in restricted source-map supplements. A multi-page artifact may still be time-smeared unless all pages came from one poll.
- **Observed state** is a process, module, thread, virtual range, or page state returned by memflow at a recorded poll.
- **Derived** means deterministic processing of exact bytes or observed state: address resolution, adjacent-version comparison, PE parsing, raw/virtual section mapping, or a conflict-excluding byte union.
- **Inferred** is the narrowest behavioral interpretation supported by several observations. It is not a direct call or I/O trace.
- **Gap** means unobserved time, a failed read, missing bytes, ambiguous/conflicting bytes, or a semantic question that this acquisition mode cannot answer.

## Bottom line

The first session observed the same three-stage order that the second session independently reproduced: `FortniteLauncher.exe` -> `FortniteClient-Win64-Shipping_EAC_EOS.exe` -> `EasyAntiCheat_EOS.exe`. All three survived through each focused watcher's final poll. The ordinary game image `FortniteClient-Win64-Shipping.exe` was present in readable launch material but was never observed as a process; that distinction must not be collapsed.

The wrapper and EAC service each held a raw-file-layout copy of the same x64 driver. Their first-session overlapping 18,621,754 bytes agreed exactly. The first-session union directly observed 44,658,328 of the driver's 44,666,520-byte declared raw extent, with two 4,096-byte holes and no conflicts. Those two pages were reconstructed from exact captured loaded-section bytes, producing SHA-256 `020d5da6b881408ced33be09b92dc45e12e641b8b3c91f3a327429e5027fef74`. Four complete, zero-failure raw captures in the later memflow session, plus that session's independent two-process union, reproduce the same size and SHA-256. Therefore the emitted file is now independently confirmed exact, while the first-session provenance remains a hybrid of 44,658,328 direct raw bytes and 8,192 loaded-to-raw-derived bytes.

The wrapper also contained two complete large private executable regions. Both were byte-identical after relocation in the second run. The 958,464-byte region has sampled thread pointers into it in both runs; the 794,624-byte region has executable-range state but no sampled thread pointer. Neither region has a valid embedded PE header.

Readable resident pages contain driver/service paths, Fortnite target paths, TPM/EK-certificate vocabulary, network/certificate material, configuration and bootstrap telemetry, and credential-bearing launch material. Presence supports state and phase association only; it does not prove a particular query, function call, registry access, packet, or IOCTL. Credential values are intentionally absent from every derived ledger.

## First-session acquisition coverage

The watcher armed at `2026-09-13T23:22:27.571Z`. Its baseline enumerated 103 processes and found no selector match or baseline error. The active focused interval runs from sequence 720 at `23:23:03.894Z` through sequence 1003 at `23:28:06.782Z`: 284 active polls over 302.888 seconds. The overall generated summary covers 1,004 timeline records and 339,213 ms from watcher start to final record. A separate stop marker is `23:28:32Z`, so the focused timeline ends 25.218 seconds before the stop marker.

The configured 50 ms interval did not become the effective active sampling period because each pass read processes and pages sequentially. Active poll durations were 332-3,365 ms, median 1,030 ms, mean 1,067.15 ms, p90 1,086.1 ms, p95 1,122 ms, and p99 2,418.32 ms. Adjacent active timestamps were 1,008-3,366 ms apart, median 1,031 ms and mean 1,070.28 ms. The four largest early blind intervals were:

| From -> to | UTC interval | Duration |
|---|---|---:|
| 721 -> 722 | `23:23:05.211Z` -> `23:23:08.577Z` | 3,366 ms |
| 722 -> 723 | `23:23:08.577Z` -> `23:23:11.787Z` | 3,210 ms |
| 724 -> 725 | `23:23:14.105Z` -> `23:23:17.018Z` | 2,913 ms |
| 723 -> 724 | `23:23:11.787Z` -> `23:23:14.105Z` | 2,318 ms |

At sequence 722 the 64 MiB new-range budget was saturated with 42 wrapper and 34 EAC ranges pending. At sequence 723 it was again saturated with 43 wrapper and 3 EAC ranges pending. Sequence 724 read 44,739,243 bytes without saturation but still ended with 3 pending ranges for each. The backlog was drained by sequence 725. Sequence 721 recorded the only page-read failures: six of 3,449 attempted wrapper pages; the collector did not log the six failed virtual addresses. There were no timeline error records.

Consequences: events shorter than roughly one second can begin and end unseen; startup coverage is materially coarser during the four intervals above; change-only page capture cannot prove that an unchanged page was not read or executed; and values within one pass are not simultaneous.

## Large private executable artifacts

| Process/base | Exact size and SHA-256 | Byte state | Execution evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Wrapper `0x17f383e1000-0x17f384cb000` | 958,464 B; `8df2667c5a3cf2358ffc09bd29db5bacaf8638fc6482cb4237a66aa7ab387812` | Complete 234-page sequence-722 capture; entropy 6.750453 bits/B; no valid embedded PE header | TIDs 5668 and 15192 point to `+0x5e06`; TID 14056 has a sampled trap RIP at `+0xcb7b4`. This is direct sampled address correlation, not a continuous execution trace. |
| Wrapper `0x17f3a42e000-0x17f3a4f0000` | 794,624 B; `6578d450f80948a88c241f5c32a35b4ba4cf07b3bd39af4e42b9875ac20d53c9` | Complete 194-page sequence-722 capture; entropy 6.510073 bits/B; no valid embedded PE header | No sampled thread pointer resolves into the region. Executable range state alone is not execution proof. |

The second run relocated these regions to `0x1e8acfe1000` and `0x1e8af02e000` and recovered the same complete sizes and SHA-256 values. It again found Win32 start `+0x5e06` within the first region, plus a sampled trap RIP at `+0x2bc95`. The second region changed collector source label from rotation to new-range, yet its exact bytes stayed identical; the source label therefore describes acquisition provenance, not mutation semantics.

Released non-page-identical first-run files and per-page provenance are under `analysis/process/generated/recovered-exact/`. Any generated file byte-identical to one sealed source page is withheld and replaced in public metadata by its canonical restricted source-page ordinal. `anonymous-execution-artifacts.json` contains the thread-correlated subset; `large-private-executable-regions.json` deliberately keeps executable memory state separate from execution proof.

## Resident PE headers and fragment boundary

Twelve valid-looking PE-header records were found in sampled anonymous/new ranges, but no candidate produced a complete mapped image. Nine are small 4-8 KiB header fragments with repeated content across processes and implausible or future timestamps. Their declared image sizes range from `0x5000` to `0x154000`; they are best described as copied, embedded, or malformed resident headers, not established module/file identities. `pe-candidates.jsonl` records exact VA, process, timestamp, architecture, declared sections, fragment boundary, and provenance for every candidate; it carries a public fragment SHA-256 only when the fragment is not byte-identical to a sealed source page, otherwise a restricted source-page ordinal.

The remaining three records are repeated observations of the driver header at the two process buffers. The 33,047,866-byte EAC `*.mapped-fragment.bin` is explicitly `time_smeared=true` and incomplete. It is not an original file and must not be used as one. The authoritative raw-file evidence is the conflict-excluding union and its offset-labelled fragments described next.

## Raw driver recovery, coverage, and tail correction

### Identity and raw layout

The common header parses as x64, COFF timestamp `2026-08-27T15:17:14Z`, preferred image base `0x140000000`, entry RVA `0x2594f8`, image size `0x2ae7000` (44,986,368), file alignment `0x200`, section alignment `0x1000`, and nine sections. The maximum section raw extent plus Security directory ends at `0x2a98e98` (44,666,520); this is the declared raw extent, not proof that an uncatalogued overlay never existed.

The exact first-session union is:

| Raw file interval | Length | Classification / artifact SHA-256 |
|---|---:|---|
| `[0x0,0x21e6fe4)` | 35,549,156 | directly observed; `157714b877e4ea037891d0e5ea003d9fcfb3fb367624e71c211c4e84055ddd48` |
| `[0x21e6fe4,0x21e7fe4)` | 4,096 | missing from raw buffers |
| `[0x21e7fe4,0x267efe4)` | 4,812,800 | directly observed; `ee0499a12a593d8102e7126be98192fb7714f7d991d5a838d1f750a86e4a0f14` |
| `[0x267efe4,0x267ffe4)` | 4,096 | missing from raw buffers |
| `[0x267ffe4,0x2a98e98)` | 4,296,372 | directly observed; `d356d807679727c62e6c62dede0474bbfeef52e3dbd2651165f53c79c49230a3` |

That is 44,658,328 direct exact bytes (99.9816596%), 8,192 missing bytes, zero conflict-excluded bytes, and 36,178 source observations. The EAC buffer begins at `0x3f5001c`; the wrapper copy begins at `0x17f3b54001c`. Their 18,621,754 comparable bytes are 100% equal: 4,546 full pages plus one common partial page, zero differing pages.

The post-stop physical snapshot found 4,858 raw-layout pages matching the canonical file, but only through raw offset `0x12ff000` (page end `0x1300000`). It therefore does **not** independently validate the tail. For `[0x1300000,0x2a98e98)`, length 24,743,576, the proper first-session classification is: 24,735,384 direct process-buffer bytes; 8,192 missing bytes; zero conflicts; 6,036 exact full pages, of which 2,222 are independently corroborated by both process copies and 6,031 match the loaded-to-raw derivative; zero have post-stop physical-raw corroboration. Lack of a post-stop hit is not evidence that the live process-buffer bytes were reused, but neither may the physical snapshot be cited as tail proof.

The two raw holes map to exact loaded-image intervals `[0x22393e4,0x223a3e4)` and `[0x26d13e4,0x26d23e4)`, with canonical source-page identities `source-page-048833` and `source-page-048834`. Inserting only those bytes yielded `analysis/process/generated/reconstructed-validated/EasyAntiCheat_EOS.sys`, size 44,666,520 and SHA-256 `020d5da6b881408ced33be09b92dc45e12e641b8b3c91f3a327429e5027fef74`. Its Authenticode PE digest is `1001323b9db779141c07e93aa86fedfeaac7a9bc75b60a151b72e5863dc24feb`, exactly the digest in signed `SpcIndirectDataContent`.

The later session removes the uncertainty about those inserted bytes and the first-session tail: its two-process direct union covers `[0x0,0x2a98e98)` completely (44,666,520/44,666,520), with 32,716 observations, no holes, and no conflicts. The two process buffers agree across 44,665,487 overlapping bytes. Four independently emitted complete raw files, two from the wrapper and two from EAC, all have zero read failures and the same SHA-256 `020d5da6...fef74`. This confirms the file bytes; it does not retroactively convert the first-run holes into direct first-run observations.

### Loaded image and raw-file distinction

The captured loaded kernel image is 44,986,368 bytes, SHA-256 `cfc623de01a9c436c239c56a016d936a9070cdd2432596c06f75a3d88ae68081`. Exact raw-to-virtual comparison gives 100% agreement for `.text`, both unnamed sections, `.rsrc`, `.reloc`, and `.pdata`; `.rdata` agrees for 96.0054% and `.data` for 83.9384%, consistent with relocation/runtime state. The six-byte PE-header difference and runtime-modified data mean the loaded image is a different representation and its whole-file hash must not substitute for the raw-file hash.

### Certificate and signer evidence

The complete `WIN_CERTIFICATE` table lies at raw offsets `[0x2a93c00,0x2a98e98)`, size 21,144, SHA-256 `191e0da9b01cf9715c58c2731834810e34c700dcba4bb59db39a43bd1c232f52`. Its 21,136-byte PKCS#7 object has SHA-256 `b8da69262ef3b4d2b52b16e6a634988de7b38a3c378943f47f372ed66cb59972` and was complete in both first-session process buffers.

`analysis/signing/authenticode-verification.json`, produced by `analysis/signing/verify_authenticode_capture.py`, is the authoritative signing ledger. It proves the exact PE digest binding; the Epic Games leaf's RSA PKCS#1 v1.5 SHA-256 CMS signature; a host-CA-anchored DigiCert chain at the verified RFC3161 time `2026-08-27T15:24:33Z`; and valid DigiCert timestamp imprint, signature, and chain. It also verifies a nested Microsoft Windows Hardware Compatibility Publisher CMS signature over the same PE digest and its Microsoft timestamp at `2026-08-27T15:35:45.526Z`. Both embedded Microsoft leaf-to-intermediate links verify, but the host CA bundle lacks Microsoft Root CA 2010, so this offline work makes no Microsoft trust-anchor or revocation-status claim.

Recovered metadata includes valid PE checksum `0x2a9a3f1`, imphash `272647242dc096a27c50c9e914b7a808`, CodeView GUID `79f520b6-91fd-c8e0-4c4c-44205044422e` age 1 and `Core.pdb`, version 3.2.0, and the single static import `FLTMGR.SYS!FltRegisterFilter`. An import is a linkage reference, not proof that the call occurred during the watch.

## Readable resident-data categories

| Derived category | String records / unique blobs / unique values | Process associations | First-last association |
|---|---:|---|---|
| Driver/service paths | 15 / 11 / 5 | EAC 16; wrapper 3 | `23:23:05.619-23:24:07.161Z` |
| Fortnite process/target paths | 111 / 90 / 41 | EAC 1; wrapper 68; launcher 58 | `23:23:03.869-23:24:07.163Z` |
| TPM provisioning/EK certificate | 552 / 140 / 110 | EAC 498; wrapper 68; launcher 15 | `23:23:04.241-23:24:07.179Z` |
| Network/URL/certificate material | 1,446 / 469 / 327 | EAC 783; wrapper 705; launcher 407 | `23:23:03.874-23:24:07.181Z` |
| EAC configuration/product metadata | 80 / 53 / 48 | wrapper 39; launcher 48 | `23:23:03.879-23:23:17.015Z` |
| Bootstrapper telemetry | 11 / 6 / 10 | wrapper 11 | `23:23:17.000-23:23:17.015Z` |
| Platform-policy messages | 69 / 60 / 18 | EAC 15; wrapper 56; launcher 14 | `23:23:03.878-23:24:07.152Z` |
| Kernel/registry paths | 1 / 1 / 1 | EAC 3; wrapper 2 | `23:23:05.761-23:23:13.214Z` |

Safe examples of observed vocabulary include `TpmCore20::RetrieveEkCertificate`, `activityTpmFetchEkCertificate`, `TpmBackedDeviceID`, a TPM `ProvisionInfo` registry path, driver/configuration paths, and bootstrap telemetry event names. These strings establish that such material was readable and resident. They do not show a call edge, result code, query order, returned certificate, network transaction, or kernel request.

## Credential exposure and data minimization

The sealed first-session source pages contain account IDs, account-derived pipe names, launch identifiers, exchange-code material including `AUTH_PASSWORD`, a signed startup token, an HTTP session cookie, and telemetry session IDs. The redacted first-run ledger contains 255 records: 38 account-derived pipe, 48 account ID, 45 exchange-code, 6 HTTP-cookie, 39 launch-auth, 40 launch-correlation, 27 signed-token, and 12 telemetry-session records. It preserves field name, captured length, canonical source-set ordinal, process/address association, and timestamp, but never the value, a value-derived digest, a raw page digest, or a raw blob path. The exact ordinal-to-page mapping is confined to a `0700` restricted supplement whose files are `0600`. The second run contains the same classes in 247 redacted records; count differences reflect sampled residency, not different schema.

Only `strings-all-redacted.jsonl`, `strings-high-signal-redacted.jsonl`, `credential-exposure-redacted.jsonl`, and the aggregate summary are published as derived string products. Unredacted source blobs remain restricted sealed evidence. The generator removes stale output before regeneration; the legacy unredacted filenames are absent from both derived directories. A sealed-source dictionary audit identified 31 distinct recognized values and found zero exact occurrences in any regenerated extracted-value field or unexpected metadata field. Matches outside value fields are limited to the intentionally preserved literal schema label `exchange_code`. Exchange/startup credentials and cookies should be revoked or rotated if still valid, access to sealed pages should be limited, and neither sealed pages nor unredacted derivatives should be uploaded publicly.

## Cross-run companion relationships and inferred data flow

High-confidence observed relationships are: identical process appearance order; identical per-role sets of named modules that ever appeared; identical EAC disappearance set (`TpmCoreProvisioning.dll`, `fwpuclnt.dll`, `webio.dll`, `WINHTTP.dll`); the same exact driver raw bytes resident in both wrapper and EAC service; the first-session named kernel-driver appearance immediately between wrapper and service startup; and the same two relocated, byte-identical wrapper private executable regions.

The narrow behavioral model supported by those observations is:

1. The launcher holds launch, account, network, and exchange material.
2. The EAC-suffixed wrapper receives or reconstructs correlated launch material, prepares two deterministic private executable components, and holds one exact raw driver copy.
3. `EasyAntiCheat_EOS.sys` becomes resident in the kernel before the EAC service is first sampled.
4. The EAC service holds a second exact raw driver copy, then transitions through TPM-provider and HTTP/network module phases while TPM/EK and network/certificate material is resident.

Steps 1-4 are companion-role inferences, not captured IPC or causality. Named-pipe strings do not prove a connection or payload. The user-mode logs do not identify the process that created the kernel service, the exact user-to-kernel transfer mechanism, IOCTL numbers/payloads, filter registration result, device handles, function calls, syscalls, packets, registry query sequence, or complete game-process lifetime. The kernel survey and static driver analysis are separate evidence domains and must be cited separately for kernel objects or static code semantics.

## Second-session lifecycle comparison

The later watcher independently observed launcher PID 2620 at `23:59:45.394Z`, wrapper PID 2184 at `23:59:45.768Z`, and EAC PID 9396 at `23:59:47.143Z`; all remained present at its `00:04:29.133Z` final poll, with minimum lifetimes 283,739, 283,365, and 281,990 ms. It produced 162 module, 63 thread, 6,257 range, and 60,265 page events; 36,670 unique blobs all verified; 12,060 adjacent sampled page transitions; and no read failure.

The nominal second-run interval was 25 ms, but its active median was still approximately 1,007 ms. Startup backlogs created 7,719 ms and 28,238 ms timestamp gaps. Consequently, the equal module sets, equal driver/private-region hashes, and equal order are stronger cross-run facts than differences in raw event counts. The wrapper's transient maximum of 686 anonymous-executable range tuples in run two fell back to the same final 55 ranges/1,970,176 bytes seen in run one; this is startup tuple churn plus sampling, not evidence for 686 persistent executable allocations.

## Reproducibility and authoritative ledgers

Regenerate and compare with:

```text
eac_process_repro=$(mktemp -d <temporary-directory>)

PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 python3 -B analysis/process/analyze_focused_watch.py \
  <private-capture-01> \
  --source-capture <private-capture-01> \
  --source-capture <private-capture-02> \
  --restricted-output "$eac_process_repro/restricted/launch-1" \
  --output "$eac_process_repro/launch-1-public" \
  --derived-page-correlation analysis/live-followup-20260913/derived-page-correlation.json \
  --raw-page-correlation analysis/live-followup-20260913/raw-prefix-page-correlation.json

PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 python3 -B analysis/process/analyze_focused_watch.py \
  <private-capture-02> \
  --source-capture <private-capture-01> \
  --source-capture <private-capture-02> \
  --restricted-output "$eac_process_repro/restricted/launch-2" \
  --output "$eac_process_repro/launch-2-public"

PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 python3 -B analysis/process/compare_focused_runs.py \
  "$eac_process_repro/launch-1-public" "$eac_process_repro/launch-2-public" \
  --output "$eac_process_repro/CROSS-RUN-COMPARISON.json"

PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 python3 -B analysis/process/audit_redaction.py \
  --pair <private-capture-01> "$eac_process_repro/launch-1-public" \
  --pair <private-capture-02> "$eac_process_repro/launch-2-public" \
  --report analysis/process/PROCESS-TIMELINE-REPORT.md \
  --output "$eac_process_repro/REDACTION-AUDIT.json"
```

The two post-stop correlation ledgers are explicit, stable, direct inputs only
to the launch-1 reconstruction. Launch 2 intentionally has no such post-stop
cross-check; output-location inference is not used.

`analysis/process/generated/EVIDENCE-INDEX.json` maps evidence class to every ledger. The primary machine-readable products are:

- `process-ledger.json`, `module-events-derived.jsonl`, `module-phases.json`, `thread-events-resolved.jsonl`, `range-summary.json`, and `timeline-change-polls.jsonl` for exhaustive observed chronology.
- `poll-coverage.json` for intervals, budgets, backlogs, failed reads, and atomicity boundaries.
- `blob-verification-ledger.jsonl` and `blob-verification-failures.json` for source-blob integrity.
- `page-content-transitions.jsonl` for every adjacent sampled page version comparison.
- `pe-candidates.jsonl`, `large-private-executable-regions.json`, and per-artifact provenance JSON for exact fragment/recovery boundaries.
- `raw-driver-buffer-recovery.json`, `raw-driver-union-source-observations.jsonl`, offset-labelled exact fragments, the sparse non-original convenience view, and the validated raw file for driver provenance.
- `resident-artifact-categories.json` and the redacted-only string/credential ledgers for readable data.
- `analysis/process/CROSS-RUN-COMPARISON.json` for role-by-role later-session confirmation.
- `analysis/process/REDACTION-AUDIT.json` for a value-free, reproducible scan of both derived directories and this report.

Each derived directory contains a self-excluding `SHA256SUMS`. The exact fragments and their provenance manifests are authoritative; `EasyAntiCheat_EOS.sys-raw-union-SPARSE-NOT-ORIGINAL.bin` fills holes with zero only for navigation and its hash is explicitly not an original-file hash.

## What remains unrecoverable from these captures

No claim of “everything that happened” is supportable from snapshot polling alone. Specifically unrecoverable are events wholly between polls, six failed first-run wrapper pages, exact ordering of reads within a poll as if simultaneous, freed/overwritten pre-observation data, direct API/function/syscall call edges, arguments and return values, packet contents and ordering, IPC payloads, creator/parent causality, execution coverage within private regions, and the lifetime of an ordinary non-EAC-suffixed game process that was never sampled. Absence from these ledgers means only “not observed under this selector and cadence,” not “did not occur.”

`appendices/ps-callbacks.md`:

```md
# Process, thread, and image callback semantics

> **Evidence appendix.** I generated this formal report from the private evidence vault. Kernel virtual addresses are deterministic aliases, host paths are scrubbed, and private binaries, memory, peers, and secrets are not distributed. Module-relative RVAs, structure offsets, counts, hashes, and semantic boundaries are preserved.

<a id="private-evidence-reference"></a>

This package is bound to loaded-image SHA-256 `cfc623de01a9c436c239c56a016d936a9070cdd2432596c06f75a3d88ae68081` and uses only offline memflow-derived evidence. It does not execute the driver or interact with the guest.

## Process callback

The exact ABI is `(PEPROCESS Process, HANDLE ProcessId, PPS_CREATE_NOTIFY_INFO CreateInfo)`. `CreateInfo != NULL` selects create; `CreateInfo == NULL` selects exit. The exit call reaches transition RVA `0x1dbef4`; its outer RCX is the qword at the original callback stack `+0x28`, not a value proven to be current process. The record lookup is `PsGetProcessId(RCX)` followed by `0xdfce7(key,pid)`. A non-null record is marked at `+0x156`, atomically marked at `+0x66c`, passed through the exact internal call chain, and finalized by `0xe0314`.

Crash-report registry writes occur only when `record+0x500` equals current process and `PsGetProcessExitStatus` is nonzero. The eight exact writes are:

- `ErrRpt_GameState`: REG_DWORD, record+0x5f8, 4 bytes
- `ErrRpt_GameCrashProductId`: REG_DWORD, record+0x63c, 4 bytes
- `ErrRpt_GameCrashPID`: REG_DWORD, stack local current PID, 4 bytes
- `ErrRpt_GameCrashCreateTime`: REG_QWORD, stack local from PsGetProcessCreateTimeQuadPart, 8 bytes
- `ErrRpt_GameCrashExitTime`: REG_QWORD, stack local derived on this branch, 8 bytes
- `ErrRpt_GameCrashExitStatus`: REG_DWORD, stack local exit status, 4 bytes
- `ErrRpt_GameCrashInfo01`: REG_DWORD, stack local, 4 bytes
- `ErrRpt_GameCrashInfo12`: REG_QWORD, record+0x3b8, 8 bytes

## Thread callback

The exact ABI is `(HANDLE ProcessId, HANDLE ThreadId, BOOLEAN Create)`. The outer callback looks up the process, calls `0x1e19c0(Create, looked-up process, literal, current process)`, separately looks up the original thread ID, and releases both successful outer lookups.

The lock-free site at `0x42e49e` is `lock cmpxchg qword ptr [record+0x4e8], replacement`. A successful CAS publishes the replacement and calls the reference-like export address; a failed CAS preserves the old slot and does not call it. `Create==1` takes the publication and create-only `record+0x370` path. `Create==0` takes `0x1b1f61` and releases its looked-up thread before `0xe0314`.

## Image callback

The exact ABI is `(PUNICODE_STRING FullImageName, HANDLE ProcessId, PIMAGE_INFO ImageInfo)`. `ImageInfo.Properties & 0x400` gates `ImageInfo+0x28` as ABI-derived `IMAGE_INFO_EX.FileObject`. The optional `0x12f2b4` result controls an owned replacement-name buffer, which is released through `0x20f604`.

`0xc2a2c` is only the final UTF-16 component extractor. On helper success, `ProcessId != 0` invokes per-process policy `0x1d872c`, compares `PsGetProcessSectionBaseAddress(current)` with `ImageInfo+0x8`, and invokes `0x1aca53` only for the main image. `ProcessId == 0` invokes bookkeeping helper `0x10844e`, excludes `\System32\Drivers\` and these five exact full names, then invokes `0x1f7f92` and `0x9af2f` for the remaining path:

- `\System32\atmfd.dll`
- `\System32\cdd.dll`
- `\System32\rdpdd.dll`
- `\System32\vga.dll`
- `\System32\workerdd.dll`

## Evidence boundary

- process: semantic identity of the qword at original callback stack +0x28
- process: protected list entry type/count behind the 0xc5bb7-dependent name path
- process: internal meanings of 0x1de875, 0x1dead6, and 0x192d2c
- thread: identity/source of the first policy-internal thread handle
- thread: identity of protected context +0x180/+0x200 values
- thread: ObfReferenceObject versus PsReferenceSiloContext alias cannot be narrowed from the shared address alone
- thread: dispatcher object type at record+0xb0 cannot be chosen from the KeReadState* alias set
- thread: release owner for a successfully published record+0x4e8 reference is outside the closed local path
- image: full effects of 0x10844e protected list/cache helper
- image: out-parameter semantics of 0xc353a before classifier 0x1f2ffc
- image: protected-state fourth argument entering 0x1d0661
- image: ownership transfer/release of PsLookupProcessByProcessId output inside 0x1aca53
- image: semantic labels for internal 0x15ed0, 0x1da3b4, 0x848b1, 0x18eab, and 0x769b7

The passive memflow callback summary and raw callback CSV independently agree on all three veneer RVAs, their raw contexts, and their direct protected-entry jumps. List membership is registration evidence only; it is not presented as callback-invocation evidence.

Validation: 20/20 checks passed. Statement coverage: 93.0% (160/172; 10 tests; threshold met). Every ledgered opcode slice and its PDATA containment/absence was re-read from the exact image and runtime-function inventory.

```

`appendices/rdtsc.md`:

```md
# Reachable RDTSC site closure

> **Evidence appendix.** I generated this formal report from the private evidence vault. Kernel virtual addresses are deterministic aliases, host paths are scrubbed, and private binaries, memory, peers, and secrets are not distributed. Module-relative RVAs, structure offsets, counts, hashes, and semantic boundaries are preserved.

<a id="private-evidence-reference"></a>

## Result

All 22 conservatively reachable RDTSC sites in the exact captured image now have byte-validated, bounded local-dataflow models. The raw image contains 1179 `0f 31` byte pairs; the 1,157 raw-only pairs are retained separately and are not promoted to instructions.

## Exact partition

- Thirteen sites execute RDTSC only if a prior 32-bit seed/state value is zero. They consume EAX only, discard EDX, mix the low 32 TSC bits, and update caller- or entry-key-indexed state. Their static presence therefore does not prove execution.
- Interpreter RVA `0x8ec5a` consumes full EDX:EAX, stores the halves as zero-extended qwords at VM-state `+0x8/+0x18`, and returns to dispatcher `0x91d54`.
- RVA `0xa8ea2` passes the full TSC qword as R8 to an unresolved entry-key-resolved callee alongside an allocated object and generated local buffer.
- RVA `0x12efac` samples once per fill-loop iteration and writes low32 or low8 fragments according to an entry-key-derived width/step relation.
- RVAs `0x16b307/0x16b501` inject EAX into two separate 32-bit candidate-search loops followed by exact boundedness and modular-witness arithmetic; the containing function finally writes two qwords to the caller result. A higher-level cryptographic object name remains unassigned.
- RVAs `0x179d8e/0x179dba` bracket CPUID and accumulate total CPUID interval cycles. RVAs `0x179de2/0x179dee` bracket adjacent RDTSC and accumulate the equal-relation baseline.

## Structured seed consumers

- Four conditional stages at `0x136545/0x1365d8/0x13662c/0x13672f` fill an exact 16-byte `4/2/2/8` layout.
- `0x1368bb` emits bounded 16-bit code units and a terminal zero word.
- `0x12ee9b` returns lower-bound plus an unsigned remainder over upper-minus-lower.
- `0x20f071/0x20f3d4/0x20f50a` seed entry-keyed table/dispatch selection state.

## Precision boundary

The ledger records exact resident bytes, PDATA containment, branches, data widths, state offsets, and in-body consumers. Entry-key-derived constants and unresolved indirect callees remain explicit. It does not claim live execution.

## Verification

- Unit tests: 8/8 pass.
- Statement coverage: 97.6%.
- Validation: `pass`.

```

`appendices/secondary-bugcheck.md`:
