IRP_MJ_SET_INFORMATION
IRP_MJ_ACQUIRE_FOR_SECTION_SYNCHRONIZATION
```

It registers no post-operation callbacks and no Filter Manager contexts.

I found four live filter instances in the captured state. The filter and instances drain during normal teardown.

## Name-policy object

The native name/access policy owns 41 records. Each record binds an extension digest, callback veneer, protected delegate or native handler, retained name state, access/counter fields, and lifecycle data.

The table includes executable, library, archive, database, text, media, image, and empty-extension cases. Some rows use common protected delegates. Others reach native mutation or unconditional-deny bodies.

The complete row-by-row table is in [the name-policy appendix](../appendices/name-policy.md).

Important correction: table membership proves that a record exists and which callback it invokes. It does not mean every system-wide file with that extension is denied. Tracked-process state, path state, record state, and protected delegate results still gate behavior.

## CREATE path

The create callback acquires normalized name information, parses it, reads desired access and internal context state, applies the name/access policy, then releases all acquired name state on the modeled exits.

The exact higher-level result depends on the record and delegate selected. Four delegate bodies remain protected beyond their entry bridge.

## Rename and link path

The set-information callback handles:

```text
10  FileRenameInformation
11  FileLinkInformation
65  FileRenameInformationEx
72  FileLinkInformationEx
```

It tracks both current and destination name state, then invokes the corresponding policy and cleanup paths. This is the right surface for observing an executable or tracked file changing identity without a new CREATE on the original name.

## Executable section synchronization

This is the most concrete file-content inspection path.

Under the recovered requestor-mode, IRQL, protection, and context gates, EAC:

1. queries standard file information;
2. requires the exact returned structure size;
3. evaluates the signed end-of-file value;
4. follows the normal positive-length path;
5. caps the read at 8 MiB;
6. allocates a buffer;
7. reads from byte offset zero;
8. passes the bytes to a classifier;
9. frees the allocation;
10. permits the section or completes with `STATUS_ACCESS_DENIED`.

I have the I/O, size, ownership, and deny skeleton. The deepest classifier state remains protected.

## Startup fallback and deny snapshot

The name policy includes a Startup-directory fallback that mutates record state when path conditions match. A separate deny-snapshot helper captures selected access and stack/frame-chain state before returning denial.

All 41 live records contain exact mutable values. Nonzero counters are captured runtime state, not assumed initialization constants.

## What this tells me

The registry and file-system layers cover several common ways untrusted code gains persistence, changes process startup behavior, alters debugger/compatibility policy, injects through shell/application surfaces, modifies network catalogs, or changes executable file identity.

I am describing enforcement mechanisms, not a list of guaranteed cheat signatures. The protected policy layer decides how these signals combine with the tracked game context.

```

`docs/remaining-work.md`:

```md
# What I Have Left

This is the handoff I would want if I came back to the project six months from now with none of the current context loaded.

The investigation is deep. It is not finished. These are the remaining problems, ordered by how much they would change the behavioral model of this build.

## P0: recover a naturally accepted device request

This is the largest open boundary.

I recovered fifteen distinct `METHOD_NEITHER`-shaped dispatcher classes, fourteen delegated handler entries, one inline path, handler register ABIs, field touches, several length boundaries, pointer splits, and selected output writes. I also recovered the namespace, link ACL, driver ACL, stable game/wrapper handles, CREATE/CLOSE behavior, and null per-handle contexts.

What I still need is one naturally occurring accepted EAC-device IRP retained in host-side memory.

The next capture must preserve:

1. caller process and thread;
2. exact `FILE_OBJECT` and granted handle access;
3. control code and transfer method;
4. input and output lengths;
5. every caller-owned, IRP-owned, and driver-owned buffer;
6. live authentication and state predicates;
7. complete packet structure by branch;
8. completion status and `Information` value;
9. all driver-state mutations and external calls;
10. cleanup and reference ownership.

Neither sealed physical sweep retained such an IRP. Static separation is not live protocol acceptance, and I will not publish a fake protocol by promoting candidate values.

## P0: identify the readiness-event creators and signalers

I have exact object identities and a useful transition:

```text
EventModule: signaled -> signaled
EventDriver: signaled -> signaled
EventGame:   unsignaled with one EAC waiter -> signaled with no waiter
```

The EAC-suffixed wrapper holds modify rights to the same game-event object. That makes it the obvious signaler candidate, but “obvious” is not evidence.

I still need:

- the create/open callsite for each named event;
- creator process and thread;
- event type and initial state;
- signal and reset callsites;
- the exact state machine tying Module, Driver, Game, shared-section readiness, device readiness, and protected-process startup together.

The next collector should combine event-object state, every handle reference, waiter stacks, WOW64 service/wrapper module maps, native API target maps, and high-cadence pointer provenance. The collector stays outside the guest.

## P0: close the shared-section consumer and integrity gate

I proved that the service and wrapper have writable views of the same 96 MiB pagefile-backed section and that the complete signed driver begins at offset `0x1c`.

I have not proved:

- which component consumes the staged bytes;
- the complete envelope and trailing-data schema;
- whether the bytes are copied, mapped, transformed, or passed to another component;
- when signature or digest validation occurs;
- whether validation is performed after the last writable staging step;
- the exact zeroization and teardown sequence.

This is an integrity question, not an invitation to build a tampering path. The useful result is a complete producer-consumer-verifier graph.

## P0: recover live Filter Manager request state

The minifilter registration, outer callbacks, name policy, extension table, executable-file read path, denial path, allocation cleanup, and four protected delegate entry points are mapped.

I still need naturally retained `FLT_CALLBACK_DATA` for:

- CREATE;
- rename/link SET_INFORMATION;
- executable acquire-for-section-synchronization.

For each, I want the exact requestor process/mode, source and destination name state, desired access, information class, file object, allow/deny result, `IoStatus`, per-record mutations, and cleanup.

No strictly validated callback-data instance survived the current samples. That is a capture blocker, not a reason to guess.

## P0: finish runtime object-access policy

The process/thread Object Manager package has closed the pre/post ABI, field offsets, normal access transforms, native helper chains, membership/insert/miss paths, reference handling, and the unreachable normal post-event tail.

The remaining blocker is runtime-populated resolver/list/table state outside the PE image. A future same-epoch capture must preserve those slots and the objects they lead to. Without that, I cannot produce a universal caller-to-access decision matrix.

## P1: explain every field in the private per-thread context

The `0x78`-byte object and its lifecycle are real. Its source-level meaning is only partially recovered.

Open fields and behaviors:

- why flags take values `0`, `1`, `0x200`, and `0x201`;
- which callback family signals or waits on each embedded event;
- the semantic meaning and update sites for byte `+0x60`;
- the meaning of tail qwords `+0x68` and `+0x70`;
- protected constructor sites for callback families without native materialization;
- the final kernel allocation/free primitives beneath EAC's wrappers.

The next pass should capture context creation and active dispatch in the same epoch, not correlate stale pool memory with later code state.

## P1: observe the bugcheck callbacks coherently

I have both registered reason records, the exact ABI, the reason-1 atomic state transition, and a large part of the reason-2 path: constant resolution, page-table self-map derivation, CR3 read/reload, present/large-page checks, and a `0x1000`-strided loop.

I do not have a real callback invocation frame or reason-specific output buffer.

Needed:

- callback-thread CR3;
- `ReasonSpecificData` and its concrete subtype;
- real loop start, end, and termination values;
- output-buffer mutations;
- registration and deregistration callsites;
- unload ordering around both records.

The current protected-state image and physical snapshots come from different load epochs. Reusing an old pool pointer under a new page table is not reconstruction; it is contamination.

## P1: close runtime resolver keys and dynamic tables

Two caller-frame-keyed resolver sites remain because their runtime RCX keys were not captured. Several Object Manager and protected paths also depend on table/list slots outside the image.

A continuation should capture the exact entry frame, key, resolved target, target ownership, and lifetime in one epoch. It should then re-run the static model with the captured state rather than hard-coding a convenient answer.

## P1: connect TPM requests to responses and consumers

The service retains exact `GetCapability` and `ReadPublic` request buffers for persistent handle `0x810eac00`. That does not prove transport, completion, or use.

I still need:

- the transport callsite;
- returned TPM response;
- public-area fields and validation result;
- owning companion component;
- comparison or serialization consumer;
- cleanup/zeroization;
- proof of whether a derived result enters the observed network connection.

The network and TPM artifacts currently coexist in process memory. Coexistence is not a data-flow edge.

## P1: reconstruct full unload and error rollback

The normal lifecycle removes the linked driver, device, minifilter, and enumerated callbacks. A supplemental same-build capture also shows filter-instance drain and a teardown dispatcher transition.

The exact order is still incomplete. I want:

- callback deregistration order;
- filter unregister and instance-drain order;
- device and symbolic-link deletion order;
- private per-thread list drain;
- event and shared-section handle closure;
- behavior with a pending request or live handle;
- rollback after each failed initialization stage;
- service/game recovery after an interrupted launch.

Normal final absence is good cleanup evidence. It is not a map of every destructor.

## P1: attribute the adjacent executable page

One launch contained a live-only 4 KiB writable/executable thunk page near the image. It had no captured direct reference from the driver and no enumerated callback or dispatcher targeted it.

Before attributing it to EAC, I need PFN ownership, allocation metadata, stored-pointer provenance, code-target references, creation timing, and unload timing. Proximity is not ownership.

## P2: finish the decompression engine

The Kraken type-0 Huffman, type-2 binary, and type-4 unary paths are closed through their seam validation. The next semantic expansion is:

- type-1 TANS;
- type-3 RLE;
- type-5 recursive decode;
- their descendants;
- two mutable optional callback thunks;
- live input/output provenance.

The static implementation is substantial. The product role is still unproven without a captured live blob.

## P2: keep eating the function census

The exact PDATA census contains 5,476 functions. Only a minority have useful semantic names. The best next targets are:

1. the largest fully decoded unnamed functions with no unresolved transfer and no direct calls;
2. the highest-unresolved protected PDATA bodies;
3. functions directly adjacent to already closed callback, IOCTL, minifilter, staging, and teardown paths;
4. functions that consume captured runtime tables or objects.

I would not optimize for raw “functions named.” I would optimize for closed data flow across important surfaces.

## P2: companion private regions and product lifecycle

The service and wrapper contained large private executable regions and protected transitions that deserve their own census. I also have not documented the entire product lifecycle across clean install, update, service start, reboot, crash recovery, uninstall, catalog validation, and version migration.

That is a separate capture program, not something to infer from one game launch.

## Rules for whoever resumes this

1. Stay memflow-only for observation.
2. Use a unique immutable output directory for every survey.
3. Hash the collector source, binary, inputs, sidecars, and outputs.
4. Match the exact Windows kernel PDB before decoding structures.
5. Write a failing invariant test before changing a collector or reducer.
6. Preserve torn links and failed reads as diagnostics.
7. Cross-check across two epochs whenever the claim permits it.
8. Never upgrade registration to invocation, a handle to a completed query, a buffer to transport, or a static branch to live execution.
9. Never merge state from different load epochs without proving the mapping relationship.
10. Seal each package only after tests, coverage, provenance, validation, and manifest verification pass.

## My definition of done

A surface is closed when I can state its inputs, structure layout, predicates, call chain, outputs, statuses, mutations, external effects, ownership, and cleanup—or when I can point to a specific captured-state blocker that makes one of those impossible.

Anything less belongs in this file, not in a victory lap.

```

`evidence/README.md`:

```md
# Public Evidence Index

I keep this directory deliberately small.

The private evidence vault contains proprietary binaries, physical-memory images, process pages, stack windows, object payloads, and restricted values. None of that is required for a reader to understand or audit the claims in this repository.

The public files preserve:

- exact build identity;
- stable executable-section identity;
- structure and function counts;
- registered-surface counts;
- scheduler/object census totals;
- validation totals;
- semantic boundaries;
- the list of generated appendices.

Kernel virtual addresses in generated appendices are replaced with deterministic aliases. Module-relative RVAs are retained because they are the useful build-specific coordinates.

Public release records:

- [Normalized finding summary](public-summary.json)
- [Validation and sealed-package summary](validation-summary.json)
- [Generated appendix inventory](generated-appendices.txt)
- `SHA256SUMS` is generated last and verifies every other published file.

```

`tools/build_manifest.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Build a deterministic SHA-256 manifest for the public tree."""

from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
from pathlib import Path


ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
OUTPUT = ROOT / "evidence" / "SHA256SUMS"


def sha256(path: Path) -> str:
    digest = hashlib.sha256()
    with path.open("rb") as stream:
        while chunk := stream.read(1024 * 1024):
            digest.update(chunk)
    return digest.hexdigest()


def main() -> None:
    files = [
        path
        for path in ROOT.rglob("*")
        if path.is_file() and ".git" not in path.parts and path != OUTPUT
    ]
    rows = [f"{sha256(path)}  {path.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix()}" for path in sorted(files)]
    OUTPUT.write_text("\n".join(rows) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

```

`tools/validate_publication.py`:

```py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Fail closed when the public research tree violates its release contract."""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import re
import sys
from pathlib import Path


ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
TEXT_SUFFIXES = {".md", ".txt", ".json", ".cff", ".py", ""}
FORBIDDEN_SUFFIXES = {".bin", ".raw", ".dmp", ".sys", ".exe", ".dll", ".pdb", ".cab"}
LOCAL_PATHS = re.compile(
    "/home/" + "cold/|/opt/" + "celestial-toolkit/|\\." + "codex/|/tmp/" + "eac-"
)
RAW_KERNEL_VA = re.compile(
    r"(?<![0-9a-fA-F])(?:0x)?ffff[0-9a-fA-F]{12}(?![0-9a-fA-F])",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)
IPV4 = re.compile(r"(?<![0-9])(?:[0-9]{1,3}\.){3}[0-9]{1,3}(?![0-9])")
TOKEN = re.compile(
    r"gho_[A-Za-z0-9]{12,}|github_pat_[A-Za-z0-9_]{12,}|"
    r"Authorization:\s*Bearer\s+\S+|eyJ[A-Za-z0-9_-]{20,}\.[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,}",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)
RAW_CAPTURE_ADDRESS = re.compile(
    r"\b(?:DTB|System CR3|physical)\s+`(?:0x)?[0-9a-fA-F]{5,16}`|"
    r"/ `[0-9a-fA-F]{9,13}` \|",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)
MARKDOWN_LINK = re.compile(r"\[[^\]]+\]\(([^)]+)\)")


def check_links(path: Path, text: str, failures: list[str]) -> None:
    for target in MARKDOWN_LINK.findall(text):
        target = target.strip()
        if not target or target.startswith(("http://", "https://", "mailto:", "#")):
            continue
        target = target.split("#", 1)[0]
        if not target:
            continue
        resolved = (path.parent / target).resolve()
        if not resolved.exists():
            failures.append(f"broken relative link: {path.relative_to(ROOT)} -> {target}")


def main() -> int:
    failures: list[str] = []
    required = [
        ROOT / "README.md",
        ROOT / "SECURITY.md",
        ROOT / "CONTRIBUTING.md",
        ROOT / "LICENSE",
        ROOT / "CITATION.cff",
        ROOT / "docs" / "remaining-work.md",
        ROOT / "evidence" / "public-summary.json",
    ]
    for path in required:
        if not path.is_file():
            failures.append(f"missing required file: {path.relative_to(ROOT)}")

    text_files = []
    word_count = 0
    for path in sorted(ROOT.rglob("*")):
        if not path.is_file() or ".git" in path.parts:
            continue
        suffix = path.suffix.lower()
        if suffix in FORBIDDEN_SUFFIXES:
            failures.append(f"forbidden artifact type: {path.relative_to(ROOT)}")
        if suffix not in TEXT_SUFFIXES and path.name not in {"LICENSE", ".gitignore"}:
            failures.append(f"unexpected non-text file: {path.relative_to(ROOT)}")
            continue
        try:
            text = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
        except UnicodeDecodeError:
            failures.append(f"non-UTF8 file: {path.relative_to(ROOT)}")
            continue
        text_files.append(path)
        word_count += len(text.split())
        if LOCAL_PATHS.search(text):
            failures.append(f"private host path: {path.relative_to(ROOT)}")
        if RAW_KERNEL_VA.search(text):
            failures.append(f"raw kernel VA: {path.relative_to(ROOT)}")
        if IPV4.search(text):
            failures.append(f"IPv4-like value: {path.relative_to(ROOT)}")
        if TOKEN.search(text):
            failures.append(f"credential/token pattern: {path.relative_to(ROOT)}")
        if RAW_CAPTURE_ADDRESS.search(text):
            failures.append(f"raw capture address: {path.relative_to(ROOT)}")
        if suffix == ".md":
            check_links(path, text, failures)
        if suffix == ".json":
            try:
                json.loads(text)
            except json.JSONDecodeError as error:
                failures.append(f"invalid JSON: {path.relative_to(ROOT)}: {error}")

    appendices = list((ROOT / "appendices").glob("*.md"))
    docs = list((ROOT / "docs").glob("*.md"))
    readme = (ROOT / "README.md").read_text(encoding="utf-8") if (ROOT / "README.md").is_file() else ""
    if len(appendices) < 25:
        failures.append(f"appendix coverage too small: {len(appendices)}")
    if len(docs) < 9:
        failures.append(f"narrative deep-dive coverage too small: {len(docs)}")
    if word_count < 40_000:
        failures.append(f"publication too small for evidence set: {word_count} words")
    first_person_markers = sum(readme.count(value) for value in ("I ", "I'", "my ", "My "))
    if first_person_markers < 25:
        failures.append(f"README voice is not consistently first-person: {first_person_markers}")
    required_phrases = [
        "The major findings",
        "How I reversed it",
        "The overload",
        "sampled absence",
        "fifteen distinct `METHOD_NEITHER`-shaped control-flow classes",
        "two kernel bugcheck-reason records and fourteen EAC-owned",
    ]
    for phrase in required_phrases:
        if phrase not in readme:
            failures.append(f"README missing canonical statement: {phrase}")

    result = {
        "schema": "eac-eos-publication-validation-v1",
        "status": "PASS" if not failures else "FAIL",
        "files_checked": len(text_files),
        "words": word_count,
        "docs": len(docs),
        "appendices": len(appendices),
        "failures": failures,
    }
    print(json.dumps(result, indent=2, sort_keys=True))
    return 0 if not failures else 1


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

```
